content: import argparse
import numpy as np
import vtk
from pyviz4d.viz import Viewer4D, TemporalActor

class OrbitingPlanet(TemporalActor):
    def __init__(self, color, radius, orbit_distance, speed):
        self.orbit_distance = orbit_distance
        self.speed = speed
        
        source = vtk.vtkSphereSource()
        source.SetRadius(radius)
        source.SetThetaResolution(30)
        source.SetPhiResolution(30)
        
        mapper = vtk.vtkPolyDataMapper()
        mapper.SetInputConnection(source.GetOutputPort())
        
        actor = vtk.vtkActor()
        actor.SetMapper(mapper)
        actor.GetProperty().SetColor(*color)
        actor.GetProperty().SetSpecular(0.4)
        
        super().__init__(actor)
        
    def update(self, current_time: float):
        angle = current_time * self.speed
        x = self.orbit_distance * np.cos(angle)
        z = self.orbit_distance * np.sin(angle)
        self.actor.SetPosition(x, 0, z)

def main():
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="PyViz4D Grid Views Demo")
    parser.add_argument("--nrows", type=int, default=1, help="Number of rows")
    parser.add_argument("--ncols", type=int, default=2, help="Number of columns")
    args = parser.parse_args()

    print(f"Creating a {args.nrows}x{args.ncols} grid layout...")
    
    # 1. Initialize Viewer4D with the grid
    viewer = Viewer4D(size=(1200, 600), bg_color=(0.1, 0.1, 0.15), nrows=args.nrows, ncols=args.ncols)
    
    # 2. Common Object: The Central Sun
    sun_source = vtk.vtkSphereSource()
    sun_source.SetRadius(2.0)
    sun_source.SetThetaResolution(50)
    sun_source.SetPhiResolution(50)
    sun_mapper = vtk.vtkPolyDataMapper()
    sun_mapper.SetInputConnection(sun_source.GetOutputPort())
    sun_actor = vtk.vtkActor()
    sun_actor.SetMapper(sun_mapper)
    sun_actor.GetProperty().SetColor(1.0, 0.8, 0.1) # Yellow-Orange Sun
    # Add to ALL views by setting view=None
    viewer.add_actor(sun_actor, view=None)

    # 3. Common Object: A bounding box to show the shared 3D space
    cube = vtk.vtkCubeSource()
    cube.SetBounds(-10, 10, -5, 5, -10, 10)
    cube_mapper = vtk.vtkPolyDataMapper()
    cube_mapper.SetInputConnection(cube.GetOutputPort())
    cube_actor = vtk.vtkActor()
    cube_actor.SetMapper(cube_mapper)
    cube_actor.GetProperty().SetRepresentationToWireframe()
    cube_actor.GetProperty().SetColor(0.4, 0.4, 0.4)
    # Add to ALL views
    viewer.add_actor(cube_actor, view=None)
    
    # 4. View-specific Objects
    # We will add an Earth-like planet exclusively to View 0 (Left/Top)
    earth = OrbitingPlanet(color=(0.2, 0.5, 1.0), radius=0.8, orbit_distance=6.0, speed=0.05)
    viewer.add_actor(earth, view=0)
    
    # And a Mars-like planet exclusively to View 1 (Right/Bottom)
    if args.nrows * args.ncols > 1:
        mars = OrbitingPlanet(color=(1.0, 0.3, 0.2), radius=0.5, orbit_distance=8.0, speed=0.03)
        viewer.add_actor(mars, view=1)
        
    # 5. Position Camera (since they are linked, manipulating the first sets it for all)
    cam = viewer.ren.GetActiveCamera()
    cam.SetPosition(0, 15, 25)
    cam.SetFocalPoint(0, 0, 0)
    cam.SetViewUp(0, 1, 0)
    
    # Ensure all renderers adjust clipping range to camera
    for ren in viewer.renderers:
        ren.ResetCameraClippingRange()
        
    viewer.add_playback_ui(max_time=300)
    
    print("Launching PyViz4D Grid Demo...")
    viewer.start(timer_interval_ms=16)

if __name__ == '__main__':
    main()

## Agent (2026-08-28 22:43:43)

I have implemented the grid view capability with synchronized camera POV and time controls, along with a runnable example!

##### What was added:

1. **Grid Layout in `Viewer4D` (`pyviz4d/viz.py`)**:
   - `Viewer4D.__init__` now accepts `nrows` and `ncols` (defaulting to 1x1).
   - Dynamically partitions the VTK render window into normalized viewports for each $(r, c)$ grid cell.
   - Automatically links the active camera across all renderers (`ren.SetActiveCamera(...)`), ensuring that panning, rotating, zooming, or resetting the camera in any view updates **all views in real time**.

2. **Selective & Common Actor Placement (`add_actor`)**:
   - `viewer.add_actor(actor, view=None)`:
     - `view=None` adds the actor to **all views** (common setup/background/bounding box).
     - `view=0`, `view=1`, or `view=(r, c)` adds the actor only to that specific grid cell.
   - Any `TemporalActor` registered in the scene is automatically stepped forward with the global time loop and slider scrubbing.

3. **Example Demo (`examples/demo_grid.py`)**:
   - Demonstrates a solar system simulation split across multiple grid views.
   - The central Sun and the bounding reference box are added as common objects to all views.
   - A blue planet is added specifically to View 0 (left/top) and a red planet to View 1 (right/bottom).
   - Moving the camera rotates and zooms both views in lockstep, and the time slider/playback steps both orbits synchronously.

##### Try the Demo:

```bash
# 1x2 side-by-side grid
uv run --with vtk --with numpy examples/demo_grid.py --nrows 1 --ncols 2

### 2x2 multi-panel layout
uv run --with vtk --with numpy examples/demo_grid.py --nrows 2 --ncols 2
```

## User (2026-08-28 22:45:14)

Summarize how this was implemented.


## Agent (2026-08-28 22:45:19)

Here is a summary of how the multi-view grid layout and camera/time synchronization were implemented in **PyViz4D**:

---

##### 1. Viewport Partitioning & Multi-Renderer Architecture (`Viewer4D`)
Instead of a single `vtkRenderer`, `Viewer4D` was extended to manage an array of renderers organized in an $(N_{rows} \times N_{cols})$ grid:
- **Normalized Viewports:** During `__init__`, the single `vtkRenderWindow` is sliced into rectangular sub-regions using VTK's `SetViewport(xmin, ymin, xmax, ymax)` in normalized coordinates $[0.0, 1.0]$.
- **Camera Linking:** For every secondary renderer created beyond the first, we call:
  ```python
  ren.SetActiveCamera(self.renderers[0].GetActiveCamera())
  ```
  Because all viewports share the exact same underlying `vtkCamera` pointer, any camera transformation (orbit, pan, zoom, reset clipping range) executed in one view instantly and natively applies to all views simultaneously with zero latency.

---

##### 2. Common vs. View-Specific Actor Routing (`add_actor`)
The `add_actor` API was updated to support targeted actor placement:
```python
def add_actor(self, actor, view=None):
```
- **Common Objects (`view=None`):** Adds the actor to all renderers in `self.renderers` (e.g., coordinate axes, central bodies, ambient reference grids).
- **View-Specific Objects (`view=int` or `view=(r, c)`):** Routes the actor exclusively to the specified grid cell's renderer.
- **Global Temporal Tracking:** If the actor is a `TemporalActor`, it is appended to `self.temporal_actors` globally so that time updates propagate regardless of which view contains the actor.

---

##### 3. Synchronized 4D Playback & UI
- **Single Master Time Loop:** `_timer_callback` and `_update_scene` advance global `current_time` and call `.update(current_time)` on all registered `TemporalActor` instances.
- **Unified Redraw:** When the scene updates, `self.ren_win.Render()` refreshes all linked viewports in a single render pass.
- **Global UI Controls:** The interactive time/speed sliders and keyboard bindings (`SPACE` for Play/Pause, `f` for Fullscreen) operate on the master window and keep all views locked in time.

---

##### 4. Verification & Example (`demo_grid.py`)
A multi-panel solar system simulation was created in `examples/demo_grid.py`:
- Shared items (the Sun and the wireframe bounding volume) are added to all views with `view=None`.
- Unique planets (Earth and Mars) are placed into specific sub-views (`view=0` and `view=1`).
- Interacting with the scene confirms that camera maneuvers and playback time scrubbing remain strictly synchronized across all panels.

## User (2026-08-28 22:49:43)

Would it make sense to add capabilty to output VTK binary of each component, and then there is a final viz step that composes the visualization? Where there have to be a binary per frame to handle time?


## Agent (2026-08-28 22:49:49)

Yes, this makes **a tremendous amount of sense** and is actually the standard architecture used in professional scientific visualization (such as in ParaView, VisIt, and Kitware workflows). 

Decoupling simulation/data generation from the final visual composition provides several massive architectural advantages:

---

##### Why Decoupling is Beneficial

1. **Decoupled Compute & Viz:**
   - Simulations (like PhiFlow, climate models, CFD, orbital mechanics) often take minutes, hours, or run on HPC clusters without a GPU or display.
   - Saving binary checkpoints lets you run the heavy math once, save the output, and iterate on visual styling (color maps, isosurface thresholds, camera angles, lighting, video export) instantly in Python without re-running the simulation.
2. **Memory Efficiency:**
   - Currently, storing hundreds of 3D grids in Python memory (`list` of NumPy arrays) exhausts RAM at high resolutions.
   - Saving to disk lets `Viewer4D` lazy-load only the active frame (or stream from SSD), allowing virtually unlimited time steps and resolution.
3. **Tool Interoperability:**
   - Standard VTK binary files can be loaded directly into ParaView, Blender, or PyVista for cross-validation or presentation-grade rendering.

---

##### How VTK Handles Time Across Binaries

In the modern VTK standard (XML-based `.vti`, `.vtp`, `.vtu`), **time-series data is handled as one file per component per time step**, tied together by a small metadata file:

###### 1. File Structure on Disk
For each simulation/actor, write binary files with a time index in the filename:
- **Image/Grid Data (3D volumes/density):** `density_00000.vti`, `density_00001.vti`, ... (using `vtkXMLImageDataWriter`)
- **PolyData (Surfaces/Particles/Lines/Streamlines):** `particles_00000.vtp`, `particles_00001.vtp`, ... (using `vtkXMLPolyDataWriter`)

###### 2. The `.pvd` (ParaView Data) or `.json` Index (Standard Composition)
VTK has a built-in time-series collection manifest format called **`.pvd`** (or a `.json` series). It is a tiny XML file that maps simulation time to the corresponding binary file:

```xml
<?xml version="1.0"?>
<VTKFile type="Collection" version="0.1" byte_order="LittleEndian">
  <Collection>
    <DataSet timestep="0.0" group="" part="0" file="density_00000.vti"/>
    <DataSet timestep="1.0" group="" part="0" file="density_00001.vti"/>
    <DataSet timestep="2.0" group="" part="0" file="density_00002.vti"/>
  </Collection>
</VTKFile>
```

#### 3. Composition Step in PyViz4D
In `pyviz4d`, we can create a `FileSeriesActor`:
```python
### Simulation Step (run once or on HPC)
exporter = VTKSeriesWriter(output_dir="sim_data/")
for t, field in enumerate(simulation):
    exporter.write_frame(field, timestep=t)

### Visualization Step (instant, interactive, multi-view)
viewer = Viewer4D(nrows=1, ncols=2)
density_actor = IsosurfaceFileSeriesActor("sim_data/density.pvd", iso_values=[0.1, 0.5, 1.0])
particles_actor = PolyDataFileSeriesActor("sim_data/streamlines.pvd")

viewer.add_actor(density_actor, view=0)
viewer.add_actor(particles_actor, view=1)
viewer.start()
```

---

### Summary
- **Do we need a binary per frame?** Yes—standard VTK architecture uses 1 binary file (`.vti` for volumes, `.vtp` for meshes/particles) per component per frame, indexed by a lightweight `.pvd` collection file.
- **Is it worth adding to `pyviz4d`?** Absolutely. It turns `pyviz4d` from an in-memory script helper into a scalable scientific pipeline capable of handling large 4D datasets.

Would you like me to implement a `VTKSeriesWriter` and a corresponding `FileSeriesActor` (or lazy-loading reader) in `pyviz4d`?

## User (2026-08-28 22:51:52)

Yes. Do it, and cleanly.

content: import os
import xml.etree.ElementTree as ET
from typing import List, Union
import numpy as np
import vtk
from .volume import create_vtk_image_from_numpy


class VTKSeriesWriter:
    """
    Exports time-series components to binary VTK XML files (.vti, .vtp)
    and maintains a .pvd (ParaView Data) collection manifest.
    """
    def __init__(self, output_dir: str, collection_name: str = "series"):
        self.output_dir = output_dir
        self.collection_name = collection_name
        self.pvd_path = os.path.join(output_dir, f"{collection_name}.pvd")
        self.entries = []  # tuples of (timestep, relative_filename)
        os.makedirs(self.output_dir, exist_ok=True)

    def write_image_data(self, data: Union[np.ndarray, vtk.vtkImageData], timestep: float,
                         filename_prefix: str = "frame", spacing=(1.0, 1.0, 1.0)):
        """
        Writes a 3D numpy array or vtkImageData as a binary .vti file and records the timestep.
        """
        if isinstance(data, np.ndarray):
            vtk_image = create_vtk_image_from_numpy(data, spacing=spacing)
        elif isinstance(data, vtk.vtkImageData):
            vtk_image = data
        else:
            raise TypeError("Data must be a 3D numpy array or vtkImageData")

        filename = f"{filename_prefix}_{len(self.entries):05d}.vti"
        full_path = os.path.join(self.output_dir, filename)

        writer = vtk.vtkXMLImageDataWriter()
        writer.SetFileName(full_path)
        writer.SetInputData(vtk_image)
        writer.SetDataModeToBinary()
        writer.Write()

        self.entries.append((timestep, filename))
        self._write_pvd()
        return full_path

    def write_poly_data(self, poly_data: vtk.vtkPolyData, timestep: float,
                        filename_prefix: str = "mesh"):
        """
        Writes a vtkPolyData object as a binary .vtp file and records the timestep.
        """
        filename = f"{filename_prefix}_{len(self.entries):05d}.vtp"
        full_path = os.path.join(self.output_dir, filename)

        writer = vtk.vtkXMLPolyDataWriter()
        writer.SetFileName(full_path)
        writer.SetInputData(poly_data)
        writer.SetDataModeToBinary()
        writer.Write()

        self.entries.append((timestep, filename))
        self._write_pvd()
        return full_path

    def _write_pvd(self):
        """Generates/updates the .pvd XML manifest."""
        root = ET.Element("VTKFile", type="Collection", version="0.1", byte_order="LittleEndian")
        collection = ET.SubElement(root, "Collection")

        for ts, fname in self.entries:
            ET.SubElement(collection, "DataSet", timestep=str(ts), group="", part="0", file=fname)

        tree = ET.ElementTree(root)
        ET.indent(tree, space="  ", level=0)
        tree.write(self.pvd_path, xml_declaration=True, encoding="utf-8")


def parse_pvd(pvd_path: str) -> List[tuple]:
    """
    Parses a .pvd file into a sorted list of (timestep, absolute_filepath).
    """
    tree = ET.parse(pvd_path)
    root = tree.getroot()
    base_dir = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(pvd_path))
    collection = root.find("Collection")
    if collection is None:
        return []

    entries = []
    for dataset in collection.findall("DataSet"):
        ts = float(dataset.get("timestep", 0.0))
        rel_file = dataset.get("file")
        abs_file = os.path.join(base_dir, rel_file)
        entries.append((ts, abs_file))

    entries.sort(key=lambda x: x[0])
    return entries

content: import numpy as np
import vtk
from .viz import TemporalActor
from .io import parse_pvd


class ImageDataSeriesActor(TemporalActor):
    """
    Base TemporalActor that lazy-loads 3D image data (.vti) from a .pvd series on disk frame by frame.
    """
    def __init__(self, pvd_path: str):
        self.entries = parse_pvd(pvd_path)
        if not self.entries:
            raise ValueError(f"No datasets found in .pvd file: {pvd_path}")

        self.reader = vtk.vtkXMLImageDataReader()
        self.reader.SetFileName(self.entries[0][1])
        self.reader.Update()

        self.current_idx = 0
        self.num_frames = len(self.entries)
        # Derived classes will attach their filter/mapper to self.reader.GetOutputPort()

    def get_output_port(self):
        return self.reader.GetOutputPort()

    def _update_file(self, current_time: float):
        frame_idx = int(current_time)
        clamped_idx = min(max(frame_idx, 0), self.num_frames - 1)
        if clamped_idx != self.current_idx:
            self.reader.SetFileName(self.entries[clamped_idx][1])
            self.reader.Modified()
            self.reader.Update()
            self.current_idx = clamped_idx


class IsosurfaceSeriesActor(ImageDataSeriesActor):
    """
    Isosurface contour actor backed by a .pvd time-series on disk.
    Memory-efficient: reads each binary .vti frame on demand.
    """
    def __init__(self, pvd_path: str, iso_values=None, colors=None, opacity=0.4):
        super().__init__(pvd_path)

        if iso_values is None:
            iso_values = [0.2, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0]

        self.contour = vtk.vtkFlyingEdges3D()
        self.contour.SetInputConnection(self.get_output_port())
        self.contour.ComputeNormalsOn()
        self.contour.ComputeScalarsOn()

        for i, val in enumerate(iso_values):
            self.contour.SetValue(i, val)

        self.mapper = vtk.vtkPolyDataMapper()
        self.mapper.SetInputConnection(self.contour.GetOutputPort())
        self.mapper.SetScalarRange(min(iso_values), max(iso_values))

        lut = vtk.vtkColorTransferFunction()
        if colors is not None:
            for val, color in zip(iso_values, colors):
                lut.AddRGBPoint(val, *color)
        else:
            lut.AddRGBPoint(iso_values[0], 0.267, 0.004, 0.329)
            lut.AddRGBPoint(iso_values[-1], 0.993, 0.906, 0.144)

        self.mapper.SetLookupTable(lut)

        actor = vtk.vtkActor()
        actor.SetMapper(self.mapper)

        prop = actor.GetProperty()
        prop.SetOpacity(opacity)
        prop.SetSpecular(0.8)
        prop.SetSpecularPower(60)
        prop.SetDiffuse(0.7)
        prop.SetAmbient(0.2)

        self.actor = actor

    def update(self, current_time: float):
        self._update_file(current_time)


class PolyDataSeriesActor(TemporalActor):
    """
    Actor that lazy-loads polygonal/particle/streamline data (.vtp) from a .pvd series on disk.
    """
    def __init__(self, pvd_path: str, color=(1.0, 1.0, 1.0), line_width=1.0, point_size=1.0):
        self.entries = parse_pvd(pvd_path)
        if not self.entries:
            raise ValueError(f"No datasets found in .pvd file: {pvd_path}")

        self.reader = vtk.vtkXMLPolyDataReader()
        self.reader.SetFileName(self.entries[0][1])
        self.reader.Update()

        self.current_idx = 0
        self.num_frames = len(self.entries)

        self.mapper = vtk.vtkPolyDataMapper()
        self.mapper.SetInputConnection(self.reader.GetOutputPort())

        actor = vtk.vtkActor()
        actor.SetMapper(self.mapper)
        actor.GetProperty().SetColor(*color)
        actor.GetProperty().SetLineWidth(line_width)
        actor.GetProperty().SetPointSize(point_size)

        super().__init__(actor)

    def update(self, current_time: float):
        frame_idx = int(current_time)
        clamped_idx = min(max(frame_idx, 0), self.num_frames - 1)
        if clamped_idx != self.current_idx:
            self.reader.SetFileName(self.entries[clamped_idx][1])
            self.reader.Modified()
            self.reader.Update()
            self.current_idx = clamped_idx

path: /Users/butala/src/pyviz4d/examples/demo_decoupled_pipeline.py
content: """
Demo showing the decoupled Pipeline architecture:
1. Stage 1 (Simulate / Generate Data): Generates 3D data and writes binary VTK series (.vti + .pvd).
2. Stage 2 (Compose & Visualize): Reads the .pvd file lazily from disk into PyViz4D.
"""
import argparse
import os
import shutil
import numpy as np
import vtk
from tqdm import trange
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt

from pyviz4d import Viewer4D, VTKSeriesWriter, IsosurfaceSeriesActor


def run_simulation_and_export(output_dir: str, res: int = 32, frames: int = 60):
    """
    Stage 1: Pure simulation/data generation step.
    Computes a synthetic dynamic 3D vortex/wave field and writes binary .vti + .pvd to disk.
    """
    print(f"\n[Stage 1] Simulating & writing {frames} VTK binary frames to '{output_dir}'...")
    os.makedirs(output_dir, exist_ok=True)
    writer = VTKSeriesWriter(output_dir=output_dir, collection_name="density_field")

    x = np.linspace(-3, 3, res)
    y = np.linspace(-3, 3, res * 2)
    z = np.linspace(-3, 3, res)
    X, Y, Z = np.meshgrid(x, y, z, indexing='ij')

    spacing = (6.0 / res, 12.0 / (res * 2), 6.0 / res)

    for t in trange(frames, desc="Exporting .vti files"):
        phase = t * 0.1
        # Dynamic rotating helical vortex field
        r = np.sqrt(X**2 + Z**2)
        theta = np.arctan2(Z, X)
        density = np.exp(-((r - 1.2 - 0.3 * np.sin(Y + phase))**2 + (Y - phase % 6 + 3)**2 * 0.2))
        density += 0.5 * np.exp(-((X - np.cos(phase))**2 + (Y * 0.5)**2 + (Z - np.sin(phase))**2))

        writer.write_image_data(density, timestep=float(t), filename_prefix="density", spacing=spacing)

    print(f"[Stage 1] Completed! PVD manifest written to: {writer.pvd_path}")
    return writer.pvd_path


def run_visualization_stage(pvd_path: str):
    """
    Stage 2: Visualization / composition step.
    Loads the .pvd on demand and interacts with the scene.
    """
    print(f"\n[Stage 2] Composing visualization from '{pvd_path}'...")
    viewer = Viewer4D(size=(1200, 800), bg_color=(0.08, 0.08, 0.1))

    # Transparency setup
    viewer.ren.SetUseDepthPeeling(1)
    viewer.ren.SetOcclusionRatio(0.1)
    viewer.ren.SetMaximumNumberOfPeels(4)
    viewer.ren_win.SetAlphaBitPlanes(1)
    viewer.ren_win.SetMultiSamples(0)

    # 1. Lazy-loaded Isosurface actor from disk
    cmap = plt.get_cmap('plasma')
    iso_values = [0.1, 0.3, 0.6, 0.9]
    colors = [cmap(v / max(iso_values))[:3] for v in iso_values]

    actor = IsosurfaceSeriesActor(
        pvd_path=pvd_path,
        iso_values=iso_values,
        colors=colors,
        opacity=0.45
    )
    viewer.add_actor(actor)

    # 2. Add bounding reference box
    cube_axes = vtk.vtkCubeAxesActor()
    cube_axes.SetBounds(0, 6, 0, 12, 0, 6)
    cube_axes.SetCamera(viewer.ren.GetActiveCamera())
    cube_axes.SetXTitle("X")
    cube_axes.SetYTitle("Y")
    cube_axes.SetZTitle("Z")
    for i in range(3):
        cube_axes.GetTitleTextProperty(i).SetColor(0.9, 0.9, 0.9)
        cube_axes.GetLabelTextProperty(i).SetColor(0.7, 0.7, 0.7)
    cube_axes.GetProperty().SetColor(0.3, 0.3, 0.3)
    viewer.add_actor(cube_axes)

    # 3. Position Camera
    cam = viewer.ren.GetActiveCamera()
    cam.SetPosition(18, 14, 18)
    cam.SetFocalPoint(3, 6, 3)
    cam.SetViewUp(0, 1, 0)
    viewer.ren.ResetCameraClippingRange()

    # 4. Attach playback UI
    viewer.add_playback_ui(max_time=actor.num_frames - 1, loop=True)

    print("Launching PyViz4D decoupled viewer...")
    viewer.start(timer_interval_ms=16)


def main():
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="PyViz4D VTK Series Export & Viz Demo")
    parser.add_argument("--output-dir", type=str, default="sim_output", help="Directory for VTK binary outputs")
    parser.add_argument("--res", type=int, default=32, help="Grid resolution")
    parser.add_argument("--frames", type=int, default=60, help="Number of frames")
    parser.add_argument("--viz-only", action="store_true", help="Skip simulation and viz existing .pvd")
    args = parser.parse_args()

    pvd_file = os.path.join(args.output_dir, "density_field.pvd")

    if not args.viz_only or not os.path.exists(pvd_file):
        pvd_file = run_simulation_and_export(args.output_dir, res=args.res, frames=args.frames)

    run_visualization_stage(pvd_file)


if __name__ == '__main__':
    main()


```
╭─────────────────────────────────────╮
│ examples/demo_decoupled_pipeline.py │
╰─────────────────────────────────────╯

@@ -0,0 +1,119 @@
+"""
+Demo showing the decoupled Pipeline architecture:
+1. Stage 1 (Simulate / Generate Data): Generates 3D data and writes binary VTK series (.vti + .pvd).
+2. Stage 2 (Compose & Visualize): Reads the .pvd file lazily from disk into PyViz4D.
+"""
+import argparse
+import os
+import shutil
+import numpy as np
+import vtk
+from tqdm import trange
+import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
+
+from pyviz4d import Viewer4D, VTKSeriesWriter, IsosurfaceSeriesActor
+
+
+def run_simulation_and_export(output_dir: str, res: int = 32, frames: int = 60):
+    """
+    Stage 1: Pure simulation/data generation step.
+    Computes a synthetic dynamic 3D vortex/wave field and writes binary .vti + .pvd to disk.
+    """
+    print(f"\n[Stage 1] Simulating & writing {frames} VTK binary frames to '{output_dir}'...")
+    os.makedirs(output_dir, exist_ok=True)
+    writer = VTKSeriesWriter(output_dir=output_dir, collection_name="density_field")
+
+    x = np.linspace(-3, 3, res)
+    y = np.linspace(-3, 3, res * 2)
+    z = np.linspace(-3, 3, res)
+    X, Y, Z = np.meshgrid(x, y, z, indexing='ij')
+
+    spacing = (6.0 / res, 12.0 / (res * 2), 6.0 / res)
+
+    for t in trange(frames, desc="Exporting .vti files"):
+        phase = t * 0.1
+        # Dynamic rotating helical vortex field
+        r = np.sqrt(X**2 + Z**2)
+        theta = np.arctan2(Z, X)
+        density = np.exp(-((r - 1.2 - 0.3 * np.sin(Y + phase))**2 + (Y - phase % 6 + 3)**2 * 0.2))
+        density += 0.5 * np.exp(-((X - np.cos(phase))**2 + (Y * 0.5)**2 + (Z - np.sin(phase))**2))
+
+        writer.write_image_data(density, timestep=float(t), filename_prefix="density", spacing=spacing)
+
+    print(f"[Stage 1] Completed! PVD manifest written to: {writer.pvd_path}")
+    return writer.pvd_path
+
+
+def run_visualization_stage(pvd_path: str):
+    """
+    Stage 2: Visualization / composition step.
+    Loads the .pvd on demand and interacts with the scene.
+    """
+    print(f"\n[Stage 2] Composing visualization from '{pvd_path}'...")
+    viewer = Viewer4D(size=(1200, 800), bg_color=(0.08, 0.08, 0.1))
+
+    # Transparency setup
+    viewer.ren.SetUseDepthPeeling(1)
+    viewer.ren.SetOcclusionRatio(0.1)
+    viewer.ren.SetMaximumNumberOfPeels(4)
+    viewer.ren_win.SetAlphaBitPlanes(1)
+    viewer.ren_win.SetMultiSamples(0)
+
+    # 1. Lazy-loaded Isosurface actor from disk
+    cmap = plt.get_cmap('plasma')
+    iso_values = [0.1, 0.3, 0.6, 0.9]
+    colors = [cmap(v / max(iso_values))[:3] for v in iso_values]
+
+    actor = IsosurfaceSeriesActor(
+        pvd_path=pvd_path,
+        iso_values=iso_values,
+        colors=colors,
+        opacity=0.45
+    )
+    viewer.add_actor(actor)
+
+    # 2. Add bounding reference box
+    cube_axes = vtk.vtkCubeAxesActor()
+    cube_axes.SetBounds(0, 6, 0, 12, 0, 6)
+    cube_axes.SetCamera(viewer.ren.GetActiveCamera())
+    cube_axes.SetXTitle("X")
+    cube_axes.SetYTitle("Y")
+    cube_axes.SetZTitle("Z")
+    for i in range(3):
+        cube_axes.GetTitleTextProperty(i).SetColor(0.9, 0.9, 0.9)
+        cube_axes.GetLabelTextProperty(i).SetColor(0.7, 0.7, 0.7)
+    cube_axes.GetProperty().SetColor(0.3, 0.3, 0.3)
+    viewer.add_actor(cube_axes)
+
+    # 3. Position Camera
+    cam = viewer.ren.GetActiveCamera()
+    cam.SetPosition(18, 14, 18)
+    cam.SetFocalPoint(3, 6, 3)
+    cam.SetViewUp(0, 1, 0)
+    viewer.ren.ResetCameraClippingRange()
+
+    # 4. Attach playback UI
+    viewer.add_playback_ui(max_time=actor.num_frames - 1, loop=True)
+
+    print("Launching PyViz4D decoupled viewer...")
+    viewer.start(timer_interval_ms=16)
+
+
+def main():
+    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="PyViz4D VTK Series Export & Viz Demo")
+    parser.add_argument("--output-dir", type=str, default="sim_output", help="Directory for VTK binary outputs")
+    parser.add_argument("--res", type=int, default=32, help="Grid resolution")
+    parser.add_argument("--frames", type=int, default=60, help="Number of frames")
+    parser.add_argument("--viz-only", action="store_true", help="Skip simulation and viz existing .pvd")
+    args = parser.parse_args()
+
+    pvd_file = os.path.join(args.output_dir, "density_field.pvd")
+
+    if not args.viz_only or not os.path.exists(pvd_file):
+        pvd_file = run_simulation_and_export(args.output_dir, res=args.res, frames=args.frames)
+
+    run_visualization_stage(pvd_file)
+
+
+if __name__ == '__main__':
+    main()
```

## Agent (2026-08-28 22:54:25)

I have implemented a clean, decoupled VTK binary export and lazy-loading series architecture for `pyviz4d`!

---

##### Key Modules Implemented

1. **`pyviz4d/io.py` (`VTKSeriesWriter` & `parse_pvd`)**:
   - **`write_image_data(data, timestep)`**: Converts 3D numpy arrays or `vtkImageData` directly into compressed binary `.vti` files per frame.
   - **`write_poly_data(poly_data, timestep)`**: Writes polygonal meshes, particles, or streamlines into binary `.vtp` files.
   - **Automatic `.pvd` Manifest**: Automatically creates and updates the standard ParaView Data XML collection (`<DataSet timestep="..." file="..."/>`) tying all binary frames together.

2. **`pyviz4d/series.py` (`IsosurfaceSeriesActor` & `PolyDataSeriesActor`)**:
   - Reads the `.pvd` manifest and **lazy-loads binary frames on-demand** from disk as time changes.
   - Drastically cuts down memory usage (RAM is only used for the currently active frame rather than the entire 4D time series).
   - Compatible with `Viewer4D`'s multi-view grid layouts, time scrubbing, and recording.

3. **`examples/demo_decoupled_pipeline.py`**:
   - Demonstrates the end-to-end two-stage workflow:
     - **Stage 1 (Simulate & Save):** Computes a dynamic 3D helical vortex field and streams 60 binary `.vti` files + `density_field.pvd` to disk.
     - **Stage 2 (Compose & Viz):** Reads the `.pvd` into `IsosurfaceSeriesActor` with transparent, glass-like styling and launches interactive playback.
   - Supports `--viz-only` to immediately inspect pre-computed data without re-running simulations.

---

##### How to Run the New Pipeline

To run both stages (simulate $\rightarrow$ save binary $\rightarrow$ compose viewer):
```bash
uv run --with vtk --with numpy --with tqdm --with matplotlib examples/demo_decoupled_pipeline.py
```

To re-open and iterate on the visual composition without re-running the simulation:
```bash
uv run --with vtk --with numpy --with tqdm --with matplotlib examples/demo_decoupled_pipeline.py --viz-only
```

## Agent (2026-08-29 10:43:27)

Here is the feasibility assessment and architectural plan for adding CityGML / 3D city model visualization to **PyViz4D**.

---

##### 1. Feasibility Assessment

**Feasibility:** **High (Very Straightforward & Elegant)**

- **Data Download with `pooch`:**
  - We already use `pooch` in `blue_marble.py`. We can download an open-source sample city dataset (e.g., sample CityGML / CityJSON LOD2 building block models from TU Delft's 3DCityDB or official open city portals like Berlin, Zurich, or New York OpenData).
- **Format Options (CityGML vs. CityJSON):**
  - **CityGML (XML-based GML):** Can be parsed either using standard `xml.etree.ElementTree` directly into VTK polygons, or via `citygml2gml` / `cjio` / `gdal`.
  - **CityJSON (JSON-based modern CityGML standard, OGC Standard):** Even easier—pure JSON parsing into `vtkPolyData` using only standard library `json` and `numpy`.
- **Coordinate Transformations (`pyproj`):**
  - CityGML datasets are typically in local projected coordinates (e.g., EPSG:25832 UTM for European cities, EPSG:2263 for NYC).
  - `pyproj` (or standard Cartesian offset centering for local street-level visualization) allows us to:
    1. Place buildings into **ECEF coordinates** on our `EarthViewer4D` globe.
    2. Or zero-center them into **Local Tangent Plane (ENU / East-North-Up)** for city-block scale rendering.

---

##### 2. Proposed Architecture & Modules

We can implement this cleanly in **two focused additions**:

1. **`pyviz4d/city.py` (New Module, ~100–140 lines of code)**:
   - **`fetch_city_sample(cache_path=...)`**: Uses `pooch` to fetch and cache a clean sample city block dataset.
   - **`parse_citygml(file_path, srs=..., origin_lat_lon=...)`**:
     - Extracts 3D building surface polygons (`gml:Polygon` / `gml:posList` or CityJSON `boundaries`).
     - Builds a single consolidated `vtkPolyData` (or multi-block for individual buildings).
     - Converts coordinates to either local coordinates (meters from center) or ECEF kilometers.
   - **`city_actor(poly_data, color=(0.8, 0.8, 0.8), opacity=1.0, wireframe_edges=True)`**:
     - Creates a VTK Actor with clean architectural styling (crisp edge lines + ambient occlusion / diffuse shading for buildings).

2. **`examples/demo_city.py` (New Example, ~50–70 lines of code)**:
   - Fetches the dataset via `pooch`.
   - Loads the city block into `Viewer4D` (or `EarthViewer4D`).
   - Demonstrates visual styling (e.g., color-coded building heights, transparent glass facades, or overlaying physical fluid smoke/dispersion from PhiFlow around the buildings).

---

##### 3. Estimate of Code and Dependencies

- **Additional Code:** **~150–200 lines total** across 1 new module and 1 example script.
- **Dependencies:**
  - `pyproj` (added to `pyproject.toml` as an optional `[project.optional-dependencies] city = ["pyproj"]`).
  - Standard library `xml.etree.ElementTree` or `json` (0 extra heavyweight dependencies).
  - `pooch` (already a project dependency).

---

##### 4. Next Step

If you'd like to proceed, I can implement `pyviz4d/city.py`, configure `pooch` to fetch an LOD2 sample city block, and create `examples/demo_city.py`. Would you like me to go ahead?
